. The last column states the number of observations dropped according to the trimming rule:
p(X_{0})<0.01
.

7
Conclusion

In this paper, we combined dynamic treatment evaluation with double machine learning under sequential selection-on-observables assumptions which avoids adhoc pre-selection of control variables. This approach appears particularly fruitful in high-dimensional data with many potential control variables. We suggested estimators for the (weighted) average effects of sequences of treatments (with the so-called controlled direct effect being a special case) based on Neyman orthogonal score functions, sample splitting, and machine learning-based plug-in estimates of conditional mean outcomes and treatment propensity scores. We demonstrated the
\sqrt{n}
-consistency and asymptotic normality of the treatment effect estimators under specific regularity conditions and analyzed their finite sample behavior in a Monte Carlo simulation. Finally, we applied our method to the Job Corps data to analyze the effects of distinct sequences of educational programs and found positive employment effects for vocational training when compared to no program participation.

Appendices

A
Proofs

A.1
Proof of Theorem 1

For the proof of Theorem 1 it is sufficient to check the conditions of Assumptions 3.1 and 3.2 from Theorem 3.1 and 3.2 and Corollary 3.2 from
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
. All bounds hold uniformly over
P\in\mathcal{P},
where
\mathcal{P}
is the set of all possible probability laws, and we omit
P
for brevity.

Define the nuisance parameters to be the vector of functions
\eta=(p^{d_{1}}(X_{0}),p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1}),\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}))
,

\nu^{Y_{2}}(D_{1},X_{0})
, with
p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})=\Pr(D_{1}=d_{1}|X_{0})
,
p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})=\Pr(D_{2}=d_{2}|D_{1},X_{0},X_{1})
,
\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})=E[Y_{2}|\underline{D}_{2},X_{0},X_{1}]
, and
\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})=\int E[Y_{2}|\underline{d}_{2},X_{0},X_{1}=x_{1}]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1},X_{0}}
, where
F_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1},X_{0}}
denotes the conditional distribution function of
X_{1}
at value
x_{1}
. The score function for the counterfactual
\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}=E[Y_{2}(\underline{d}_{2})]
is given by:

\displaystyle\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta,\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{n}
be the set fo all
\eta=(p^{d_{1}},p^{d_{2}},\mu^{Y_{2}},\nu^{Y_{2}})
consisting of
P
-square integrable functions
p^{d_{1}},p^{d_{2}},\mu^{Y_{2}}
and
\nu^{Y_{2}}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\eta-\eta_{0}\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C,

(A.1)

\displaystyle\left\|\eta-\eta_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n},

\displaystyle\left\|p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-1/2\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1/2-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\left\|p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})-1/2)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1/2-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-p^{d_{1}}_{0}(X_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})-p^{d_{2}}_{0}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-p^{d_{1}}_{0}(X_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}.

We furthermore replace the sequence
(\delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
by
(\delta_{n}^{\prime})_{n\geq 1},
where
\delta_{n}^{\prime}=C_{\epsilon}\max(\delta_{n},n^{-1/2}),
where
C_{\epsilon}
is sufficiently large constant that only depends on
C
and
\epsilon.

Assumption 3.1: Linear scores and Neyman orthogonality

Assumption 3.1(a)

Moment Condition:
The moment condition
E\Big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta_{0},\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0})\Big]=0
is satisfied, which follows from the law of iterated expectations:

\displaystyle E\Big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta_{0},\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\overbrace{E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p^{d_{1}}_{0}(X_{0})\cdot p^{d_{2}}_{0}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]\Bigg|X_{0}\Bigg]}^{=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\ E\Bigg[\overbrace{E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\Bigg|X_{0}\Bigg]}^{=\int E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}}=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\ E\big[\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\big]\ \ -\ \ \Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}\ \ =\ \ \Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}\ \ -\ \ \Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}\ \ =0

To better see this result, note that

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]\Bigg|X_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]\Bigg|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]\Bigg|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\Bigg]\Bigg|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0,

where the first and third equalities follow from basic probability theory and the second from the law of iterated expectations.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})]}{p^{d_{1}}_{0}(x_{0})}\Bigg|X_{0}=x_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})=0.

where the first equality follows from basic probability theory, the second from conditioning on and integrating over
X_{1}
, and the third from the fact that
\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})
is not a function of
X_{1}
.

Assumption 3.1(b)

Linearity:
The score
\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta_{0},\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0})
is linear in
\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}
:

\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta_{0},\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0})=\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{a}(W,\eta_{0})\cdot\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}+\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{b}(W,\eta_{0})

with
\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{a}(W,\eta_{0})=-1
and

\displaystyle\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{b}(W,\eta_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}+\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0}).

Assumption 3.1(c)

Continuity:

The expression for the second Gateaux derivative of a map
\eta\mapsto E[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta,\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}})]
is continuous.

Assumption 3.1(d)

Neyman Orthogonality
: For any
\eta\in\mathcal{T}_{N},
the Gateaux derivative in the direction
\eta-\eta_{0}=(p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0}),p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})-p_{0}^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1}),\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0}),\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0}))
is given by:

\displaystyle\partial E\big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta,\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}})\big]\big[\eta-\eta_{0}\big]=

\displaystyle-E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg]

(
*
)

\displaystyle+E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\Bigg]

(
**
)

\displaystyle-\ E\Bigg[\frac{\overbrace{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot\frac{[p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\Bigg]

\displaystyle-\ E\Bigg[\overbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=\int E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}}=0}\cdot\frac{[p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\Bigg]

\displaystyle-\ E\Bigg[\frac{\overbrace{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot\frac{[p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})-p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\ E\Bigg[\underbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}}_{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=\frac{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\cdot[\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}\Bigg]+E[\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}_{=0}=0

The Gateaux derivative is zero because expressions
(*)
and
(**)
cancel out. To see this, note that

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p^{d_{1}}(x_{0})}\Bigg|X_{0}=x_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\big[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int E\big[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}},

where the first equality follows from basic probability theory and the second from conditioning on and integrating over
X_{1}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p^{d_{1}}(x_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg|X_{0}=x_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{x}_{1})}\Bigg|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{x}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\Bigg]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int E\big[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},D_{2}=d_{2},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int E\big[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}},

where the first equality follows from basic probability theory, the second from conditioning on and integrating over
X_{1}
, the third from basic probability theory, and the fourth from simplification as
\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})=E[Y_{2}|D_{1}=d_{1},D_{2}=d_{2},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}]
is already conditional on
D_{2}=d_{2}
.

\displaystyle\partial E\big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta,\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}})\big]\big[\eta-\eta_{0}\big]=0

proving that the score function is orthogonal.

Assumption 3.1(e)

Singular values of
E[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{a}(W;\eta_{0})]
are bounded:

Holds trivially, because
\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{a}(W;\eta_{0})=-1.

Assumption 3.2: Score regularity and quality of nuisance parameter estimators

Assumption 3.2(a)

This assumption directly follows from the construction of the set
\mathcal{T}_{n}
and the regularity conditions (Assumption 4).

Assumption 3.2(b)

Bound for
m_{n}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(E\left[\left|\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right|^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\underline{d}_{2}\in\{0,1,...,Q\}^{2}}E\left[\left|\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right|^{q}\Pr_{P}(\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2}|\underline{X}_{1})\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2/q}\left(\sum_{\underline{d}_{2}\in\{0,1,...,Q\}^{2}}E\left[\left|\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right|^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2/q}\left(\max_{\underline{d}_{2}\in\{0,1,...,Q\}^{2}}E\left[\left|\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right|^{q}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2/q}\left(\max_{\underline{d}_{2}\in\{0,1,...,Q\}^{2}}\left\|\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}\right),

where the first equality follows from definition, the second from the law of total probability, and the third line from the fact that
\Pr(\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2}|\underline{X}_{1})=p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\geq\epsilon^{2}.
Similarly, we obtain

\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\geq\epsilon^{2/q}\left(\max_{\underline{d}_{2}\in\{0,1,...,Q\}^{2}}\left\|\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\right).

Notice that by Jensen’s inequality
\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}\leq\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}
and

\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\leq\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}
and hence

\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}\leq C/\epsilon^{2/q}
and

\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\leq C/\epsilon^{2/q},

by conditions (
A.1
). Similarly, for any
\eta\in\mathcal{T}_{N}:

\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}\leq C/\epsilon^{2/q}
and

\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\leq C/\epsilon^{2/q},
because

\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}\leq C
and

\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\leq C.

Consider

\displaystyle E\Big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta,\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2}})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\underbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot Y_{2}}_{=I_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\cdot\bigg(1-\frac{I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\bigg)\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})}_{=I_{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigg(1-\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\bigg)\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})}_{=I_{3}}-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}\Bigg]

and thus

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta,\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2}})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|I_{1}\right\|_{q}+\left\|I_{2}\right\|_{q}+\left\|I_{3}\right\|_{q}+\left\|\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}+\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}}\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon}\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}+|\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2/q}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right),

because of triangular inequality and because the following set of inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}+\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}\leq 2C/\epsilon^{2/q},

(A.2)

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}+\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\leq 2C/\epsilon^{2/q},

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|E[\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]|\leq E\Big[\left|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right|^{1}\Big]^{\frac{1}{1}}=\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{2}/\epsilon^{2/2}\overbrace{\leq}^{q>2}\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}/\epsilon\leq C/\epsilon.

which gives the upper bound on
m_{n}
in Assumption 3.2(b) of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
.

Bound for
m^{\prime}_{n}
:

Notice that

\Big(E[|\psi_{a}^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta)|^{q}]\Big)^{1/q}=1

and this gives the upper bound on
m^{\prime}_{n}
in Assumption 3.2(b).

Assumption 3.2(c)

In the following, we omit arguments for the sake of brevity and use
p^{d_{1}}=p^{d_{1}}(X_{0}),p^{d_{2}}=p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}),\nu^{Y_{2}}=\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0}),\mu^{Y_{2}}=\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})
and similarly for
p^{d_{1}}_{0},p^{d_{2}}_{0},\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0},\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}.

Bound for
r_{n}
:

For any
\eta=(p^{d_{1}},p^{d_{2}},\mu^{Y_{2}},\nu^{Y_{2}})
we have

\Big|E\Big(\psi_{a}^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta)-\psi_{a}^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta_{0})\Big)\Big|=|1-1|=0\leq\delta^{\prime}_{N},

and thus we have the bound on
r_{n}
from Assumption 3.2(c).

Bound for
r^{\prime}_{n}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta,\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2}})-\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta_{0},\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2}})\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot Y\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}

(A.3)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\left(\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\left(\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\left(\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|Y\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\delta_{n}\left(1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)+\delta_{n}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{5}}+C+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\right)+\delta_{n}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)+\delta_{n}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)+\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon}\leq\delta_{n}^{\prime}

as long as
C_{\epsilon}
in the definition of
\delta_{n}^{\prime}
is sufficiently large. This gives the bound on
r^{\prime}_{n}
from Assumption 3.2(c).
Here we made use of the fact that
\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}\leq\delta_{n}/\epsilon,

\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\leq\delta_{n}/\epsilon
and
\left\|p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}=\left\|p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},X_{0})-p^{d_{2}}_{0}(d_{1},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\leq\delta_{n}/\epsilon
using similar steps as in Assumption 3.1(b).

The last inequality in (
A.3
) is satisfied because we can bound the first term by

\displaystyle\left\|Y\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\frac{1}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}+p_{0}^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}-p_{0}^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}(p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}})\right\|_{2}+\left\|p_{0}^{d_{2}}(p_{0}^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}})\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\left\|p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\delta_{n}\left(1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right),

where the first inequality follows from the second inequality in Assumption 4(a). The second term in (
A.3
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}})\right\|_{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}+C\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon}+C\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq\delta_{n}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{5}}+C+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\right),

where the third inequality follows from
E[Y^{2}_{2}|D_{1}=d_{1},D_{2}=d_{2},\underline{X_{1}}]\geq(E[Y_{2}|D_{1}=d_{1},D_{2}=d_{2},\underline{X_{1}}])^{2}=\mu^{2}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X_{1}})
by the conditional Jensen’s inequality and therefore
\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{\infty}\leq C^{2}.

For the third term we get

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+p_{0}^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-p_{0}^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(p_{0}^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}})\right\|_{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}+C\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq\delta_{n}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\right),

and similarly, for the fourth term we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\delta_{n}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\right),

where we used Jensen’s inequality twice to get
\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{\infty}\leq C^{2}
.

Bound for
\lambda^{\prime}_{n}
:

Now consider

f(r):=E[\psi(W;\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2}},\eta+r(\eta-\eta_{0})].

For any
r\in(0,1):

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}f(r)}{\partial r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}(-2)\frac{(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]

(A.4)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}(-2)\frac{(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}2\frac{(Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{3}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}2\frac{(Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)^{3}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}2\frac{(Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}2\frac{(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{3}}\Bigg]

Note that because

\displaystyle E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|D_{1}=d_{1},D_{2}=d_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle|p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0}|\leq 2,\ \ \ \ \ \ |p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{q}\leq\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}/\epsilon^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C/\epsilon^{2/q}

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{q}\leq\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}/\epsilon^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}/\epsilon.

we get that for some constant

that only depends on

and
\epsilon

\left|\frac{\partial^{2}f(r)}{\partial r^{2}}\right|\leq C_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}\leq\delta_{n}^{\prime}n^{-1/2}

and this gives the upper bound on
\lambda^{\prime}_{n}
in Assumption 3.2(c) of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
as long as

. We used the following inequalities

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}/\epsilon

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}/\epsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\left\|p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},X_{0})-p^{d_{2}}_{0}(d_{1},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},X_{0})-p^{d_{2}}_{0}(D_{1},X_{0})\right\|_{2}/\epsilon,

and these can be shown using similar steps as in Assumption 3.1(b).

To verify that
\left|\frac{\partial^{2}f(r)}{\partial r^{2}}\right|\leq C_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}
holds, note that by the triangular inequality it is sufficient to bound the absolute value of each of the ten terms in (
A.4
) separately. We illustrate it for the first, third, and last terms. For the first term:

\displaystyle\left|E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}(-2)\frac{(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left|E\Bigg[\frac{(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\epsilon^{3}}\left|E\Bigg[(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\Bigg]\right|\leq\frac{2}{\epsilon^{3}}\frac{\delta_{N}}{\epsilon}n^{-1/2}.

For the second inequality we used the fact that for
i\in\{1,2\}:1\geq p^{d_{i}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{i}}-p^{d_{i}}_{0})=(1-r)p^{d_{i}}_{0}+rp^{d_{i}}\geq(1-r)\epsilon+r\epsilon=\epsilon
and in the third Holder’s inequality. For the third term, we get

\displaystyle\left|E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}2\frac{(Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{3}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\epsilon^{4}}\left|E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}(Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8}{\epsilon^{4}}\left|E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}(Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\Bigg]\right|+\frac{2}{\epsilon^{4}}\left|E\Bigg[r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\cdot 2}{\epsilon^{4}}\left|E\Bigg[1\cdot(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\Bigg]\right|\leq\frac{4}{\epsilon^{4}}\frac{\delta_{N}}{\epsilon}n^{-1/2},

where in addition we made use of conditions (
A.1
).

For the last term, we have

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}2\frac{(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{3}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\overbrace{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\frac{(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})}{p^{d_{1}}_{0}}}^{\int E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}=0}\cdot\frac{2p^{d_{1}}_{0}(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{3}}\Bigg]=0.

The remaining terms in (
A.4
) are bounded similarly.

Assumption 3.2(d)

\displaystyle E\Big[(\psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}(W,\eta_{0},\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2}}))^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\Bigg(\underbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}}_{=I_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}}_{=I_{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}}_{=I_{3}}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[I_{1}^{2}+I_{2}^{2}+I_{3}^{2}]\geq E[I^{2}_{1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}E\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}c^{2}}{(1-\epsilon)^{4}}>0.

because
\Pr(\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2}|\underline{X}_{1})=p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\geq\epsilon^{2},\ p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\leq 1-\epsilon
and
p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\leq 1-\epsilon.

where the second equality follows from

\displaystyle E\Big[I_{1}\cdot I_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\overbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{(p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0}))^{2}\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]\Bigg],

\displaystyle E\Big[I_{2}\cdot I_{3}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\overbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}^{E[\cdot|\underline{X}_{0}]=\int E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}=0}\cdot[\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}]\Bigg],

\displaystyle E\Big[I_{1}\cdot I_{3}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\overbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}\cdot[\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}]\Bigg].

A.2
Proof of Theorem 2

The proof follows in a similar manner than the one of Theorem 1 (Section
A.1
). All bounds hold uniformly over all probability laws
P\in\mathcal{P}
where
\mathcal{P}
is the set of all possible probability laws, and we omit
P
for brevity.

Denote by
S
a binary indicator for being selected into the target population and by
g(X_{0})=\Pr(S=1|X_{0})
the selection probability as a function of
X_{0}
.
Define the nuisance parameter to be
\chi=(g,\eta)=(g(X_{0}),p^{d_{1}}(X_{0}),p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1}),\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}),\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0}))
. The score function for the weighted counterfactual
\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}=E[Y_{2}(\underline{d}_{2})|S=1]=E[g(X_{0})\cdot Y_{2}(\underline{d}_{2})/\Pr(S=1)]
is given by:

\displaystyle\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi,\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{S}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}.

Let
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{n}
be the set fo all
\chi=(g,\eta)=(g,p^{d_{1}},p^{d_{2}},\mu^{Y_{2}},\nu^{Y_{2}})
consisting of
P
-square integrable functions
g,p^{d_{1}},p^{d_{2}},\mu^{Y_{2}}
and
\nu^{Y_{2}}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\chi-\chi_{0}\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C,

(A.5)

\displaystyle\left\|\chi-\chi_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n},

\displaystyle\left\|g(X_{0})-1/2\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1/2-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\left\|p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-1/2\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1/2-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\left\|p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})-1/2)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1/2-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-p^{d_{1}}_{0}(X_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{2}}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})-p^{d_{2}}_{0}(D_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})-p^{d_{1}}_{0}(X_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}.

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|g(X_{0})-g_{0}(X_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{D}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}\times\left\|g(X_{0})-g_{0}(X_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}.

We furthermore replace the sequence
(\delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
by
(\delta_{n}^{\prime})_{n\geq 1},
where
\delta_{n}^{\prime}=C_{\epsilon}\max(\delta_{n},n^{-1/2}),
where
C_{\epsilon}
is sufficiently large constant that only depends on
C
and
\epsilon.

Assumption 3.1: Linear scores and Neyman orthogonality

Assumption 3.1(a)

Moment Condition:

The moment condition
E\Big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi_{0},\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0})\Big]=0
holds by the law of iterated expectations:

\displaystyle E\Big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi_{0},\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\overbrace{E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]\Bigg|\underline{X}_{1}\Bigg]}^{=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\ E\Bigg[\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\overbrace{E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\Bigg|X_{0}\Bigg]}^{=\int E\Big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\ E\Bigg[\frac{S}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\Bigg]-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}\ \ =\ \ \Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}\ \ -\ \ \Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}\ \ =0.

Assumption 3.1(b)

Linearity:
The score
\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi_{0},\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0})
is linear in
\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}
:

with
\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{a}(W,\chi_{0})=-1
and

\displaystyle\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{b}(W,\chi_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{S}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0}).

Assumption 3.1(c)

Continuity:

We may observe that the expression for the second Gateaux derivative of a map
\chi\mapsto E[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi,\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1})]
, given in (
A.6
), is continuous.

Assumption 3.2: Score regularity and quality of nuisance parameter estimators

Assumption 3.2(a)

This assumption directly follows from the defition of
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{n}
and the regularity conditions (Assumption 5).

Assumption 3.2(b)

Bounds for
m_{n}
:

We start by rearranging the terms in the Neyman score function (
A.2
)

\displaystyle E\Big[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi,\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\underbrace{\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot Y_{2}}_{=I_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\cdot\bigg(1-\frac{I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\bigg)\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})}_{=I_{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigg(\frac{S}{\Pr(S=1)}-\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{p^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\bigg)\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})}_{=I_{3}}-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}\Bigg]

and then, Following the same steps as in (
A.1
), we get

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi,\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|I_{1}\right\|_{q}+\left\|I_{2}\right\|_{q}+\left\|I_{3}\right\|_{q}+\left\|\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}+\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon^{3}}\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon^{2}}\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}+|\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2/q}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)

because of triangular inequality and because the following set of inequalities hold (similarly to (
A.2
)):

\displaystyle\left\|\mu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C/\epsilon^{2/q},\ \ \left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{q}\leq 2C/\epsilon^{2/q},

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}_{0}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|E\left[\frac{S}{\Pr(S=1)}\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right]\right|\leq E\Big[\left|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right|^{1}\Big]^{\frac{1}{1}}/\epsilon=\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{1}/\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})\right\|_{2}/\epsilon\leq\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{2}/\epsilon^{4/2}\overbrace{\leq}^{q>2}\left\|Y_{2}\right\|_{q}/\epsilon^{2}\leq C/\epsilon^{2}.

which gives the upper bound on
m_{n}
in Assumption 3.2(b) of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
.

Bounds for
m^{\prime}_{n}
:

Notice that

\Big(E[|\psi_{a}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi)|^{q}]\Big)^{1/q}=1

and this gives the upper bound on
m^{\prime}_{n}
in Assumption 3.2(b) of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
.

Assumption 3.2(c)

Bound for
r_{n}
:

For any
\chi=(g,p^{d_{1}},p^{d_{2}},\mu^{Y_{2}},\nu^{Y_{2}})
we have

\Big|E\Big(\psi_{a}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi)-\psi_{a}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi_{0})\Big)\Big|=|1-1|=0\leq\delta^{\prime}_{N},

and thus we have the bound on
r_{n}
from Assumption 3.2(c) of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
.

Bound for
r^{\prime}_{n}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi,\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1})-\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi_{0},\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1})\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot Y\cdot\left(\frac{g}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{g_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\left(\frac{g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\left(\frac{g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\left(\frac{g\cdot\nu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{S}{\Pr(S=1)}(\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|Y\cdot\left(\frac{g}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{g_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|\frac{g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|\frac{g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|\frac{g\cdot\nu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{5}}\delta_{n}\left(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)+\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon^{5}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+2C+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\right)+\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+2C\right)+\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+2C\right)+\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon^{2}}\leq\delta_{n}^{\prime}

as long as
C_{\epsilon}
in the definition of
\delta_{n}^{\prime}
is sufficiently large. This gives the bound on
r^{\prime}_{n}
from Assumption 3.2(c) of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
.

The last inequality holds because we can bound the first term by

\displaystyle\left\|Y\cdot\left(\frac{g}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{g_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\frac{g}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{g_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}g_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}g_{0}+p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g-p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g_{0}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\left\|g-g_{0}\right\|_{2}+1\cdot\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\delta_{n}+1\cdot\delta_{n}\left(1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)\right)\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{4}}\delta_{n}\left(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right),

the second term is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}(g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\right\|_{2}+\left\|g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}})\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\left\|g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}+1\cdot C\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\delta_{n}\left(C+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)+C\delta_{n}\left(1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)\right)=\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon^{4}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+2C+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\right)

where
\left\|g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}
is bounded similarly as
\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}
and we also used bounds for
\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}p_{0}^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}p^{d_{2}}\right\|_{2}
derived in section (
A.1
).

while for the third term we get

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-p^{d_{1}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+p_{0}^{d_{1}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}-p_{0}^{d_{1}}g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}(g\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}-g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\right\|_{2}+\left\|g_{0}\cdot\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}(p_{0}^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}})\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\delta_{n}\left(C+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)+C\left\|p_{0}^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\delta_{n}\left(C+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)+C\delta_{n}\right)=\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+2C\right).

and similarly, for the fourth term we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}}{p^{d_{1}}}-\frac{\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\delta_{n}}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+2C\right).

Bound for
\lambda^{\prime}_{n}
:

Now consider

f(r):=E[\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W;\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1},\chi_{0}+r(\chi-\chi_{0})]

For any
r\in(0,1):

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}f(r)}{\partial r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2(g-g_{0})\Big((\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})-(\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\Big)}{p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}\Bigg]

(A.6)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{(-2)\Big(g_{0}+r(g-g_{0})\Big)\Big((\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})-(\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\Big)(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2(g-g_{0})\Big((\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))-(\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))\Big)}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2\Big(g_{0}+r(g-g_{0})\Big)\Big((\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))-(\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))\Big)(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{3}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{(-2)(g-g_{0})\Big(Y-(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))\Big)(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{(-2)(g-g_{0})\Big(Y-(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))\Big)(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{(-2)(g-g_{0})(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2\Big(g_{0}+r(g-g_{0})\Big)\Big(Y-(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))\Big)(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{3}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2\Big(g_{0}+r(g-g_{0})\Big)\Big(Y-(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))\Big)(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})^{2}}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)^{3}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2\Big(g_{0}+r(g-g_{0})\Big)\Big(Y-(\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}+r(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0}))\Big)(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2\Big(g_{0}+r(g-g_{0})\Big)(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)^{2}\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2\Big(g_{0}+r(g-g_{0})\Big)(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})}{\left(p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})\right)\left(p^{d_{2}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{2}}-p^{d_{2}}_{0})\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

here we follow the same procedure as in bounding (
A.4
), with the only exception that now we have to make use of the last two inequalities from the regularity conditions (
A.5
).

As an example, consider the first term:

\displaystyle\left|E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2(g-g_{0})\Big((\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})-(\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})\Big)}{p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2(g-g_{0})(\mu^{Y_{2}}-\mu^{Y_{2}}_{0})}{p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left|E\Bigg[\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{2(g-g_{0})(\nu^{Y_{2}}-\nu^{Y_{2}}_{0})}{p^{d_{1}}_{0}+r(p^{d_{1}}-p^{d_{1}}_{0})}\Bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{\delta_{N}}{\epsilon}n^{-1/2}+\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{\delta_{N}}{\epsilon}n^{-1/2}=4\frac{\delta_{N}}{\epsilon^{3}}n^{-1/2}.

We may bound all the remaining terms similarly and get that for some constant
C_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}
that only depends on
C
and
\epsilon

\left|\frac{\partial^{2}f(r)}{\partial r^{2}}\right|\leq C_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}\leq\delta_{n}^{\prime}n^{-1/2}

and this gives the upper bound on
\lambda^{\prime}_{n}
in Assumption 3.2(c) of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, Demirer, Duflo, Hansen, Newey, and
Robins 2018
as long as
C_{\epsilon}\geq C_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}
.

Assumption 3.2(d)

\displaystyle E\Big[(\psi^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}(W,\chi_{0},\Psi_{0}^{\underline{d}_{2},S=1}))^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\Bigg(\underbrace{\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}}_{=I_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}}_{=I_{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{S}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}}_{=I_{3}}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[I_{1}^{2}+I_{2}^{2}+I_{3}^{2}]\geq E[I^{2}_{1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\cdot I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{1-\epsilon}E\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{3}c^{2}}{(1-\epsilon)^{5}}>0.

because
\Pr(\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2}|\underline{X}_{1})=p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\geq\epsilon^{2}
,
p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})\leq 1-\epsilon
and
g(X_{0})\geq\epsilon.

where the second equality follows from

\displaystyle E\Big[I_{1}\cdot I_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\left(\frac{g(X_{0})}{\Pr(S=1)}\right)^{2}\cdot\overbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{(p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0}))^{2}\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]\Bigg],

\displaystyle E\Big[I_{2}\cdot I_{3}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{g(X_{0})\cdot S}{\left(\Pr(S=1)\right)^{2}}\cdot\overbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})}\cdot[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})]}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=\int E\big[\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{x}_{1})-\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},x_{0})\big|D_{1}=d_{1},\underline{X}_{1}=\underline{x}_{1}\big]dF_{X_{1}=x_{1}|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}=x_{0}}=0}\cdot[\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}]\Bigg],

\displaystyle E\Big[I_{1}\cdot I_{3}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Bigg[\frac{g(X_{0})\cdot S}{\left(\Pr(S=1)\right)^{2}}\cdot\overbrace{\frac{I\{D_{1}=d_{1}\}\cdot I\{D_{2}=d_{2}\}}{p_{0}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})\cdot p_{0}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})}\cdot[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})]}^{E[\cdot|X_{0}]=E[E[Y_{2}-\mu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1})|\underline{D}_{2}=\underline{d}_{2},\underline{X}_{1}]|D_{1}=d_{1},X_{0}]=0}\cdot[\nu_{0}^{Y_{2}}(\underline{d}_{2},X_{0})-\Psi^{\underline{d}_{2}}_{0}]\Bigg].

B
Covariates

Table
B.1
provides information on the covariates in our empirical application across treatment sequences. The first two columns contain the name and a description of each variable. The variable type in the third column can take the value 1, 2, or 3, which stands for dummy, categorical, or continuous variable, respectively. The fourth column presents an indicator which is 0 for covariates observed prior to the first treatment (
X_{0}
) and 1 for covariates observed after the first treatment (
X_{1}
). The number of missing values is given in the fifth column, and columns 6-15 display the mean values of the covariates for the treatment sequences in Table
3
.

C
Propensity score plots

The following figures display the overlap of the sequential treatment propensity scores across treatment states in the empirical application by means of kernel density plots. Each figure is divided into four windows. The upper windows show the first and second period propensity scores
\hat{p}^{d_{1}}(X_{0})
and
\hat{p}^{d_{2}}(d_{1},\underline{X}_{1})
under the treatment sequence. The bottom windows display the overlap for the corresponding first and second period propensity scores under the control sequence.

Figure C.0.0.1:
Support for treatment sequences 33 vs. 22 with a trimming threshold of 0.01

Figure C.0.0.2:
Support for treatment sequences 33 vs. 21 with a trimming threshold of 0.01

Figure C.0.0.3:
Support for treatment sequences 33 vs. 11 with a trimming threshold of 0.01

Figure C.0.0.4:
Support for treatment sequences 33 vs. 22 with a trimming threshold of 0.03

Figure C.0.0.5:
Support for treatment sequences 33 vs. 21 with a trimming threshold of 0.03

Figure C.0.0.6:
Support for treatment sequences 33 vs. 11 with a trimming threshold of 0.03
